NOT for payment/auction mechanism design (nisan-et-al-2007-algorithmic-game-theory), aligned agents, or implementation of file claims and locks. - **game-theoretic-analysis**: Apply game-theoretic analysis whenever multiple agents, actors, teams, or systems interact strategically — where each party's best move depends on what the others do. Triggers on "what's their incentive?", "why would they cooperate?", "how do we align incentives?", "coordination problem", "trust problem", or any situation involving negotiation, competition, cooperation under self-interest, or multi-agent interaction. Also trigger when designing multi-agent AI systems, agent negotiation protocols, or when a design fails because agents optimize for own payoffs rather than the system goal. ([AndurilCode/craftwork](https://github.com/AndurilCode/craftwork/tree/HEAD/skills/game-theoretic-analysis) · ★8)
- **game-theoretic-consensus-control**: H-infinity leader-following consensus control using coalitional zero-sum game theory and differential games. - **game-theoretic-payoff-matrix-analysis**: Use when the analysis goal is to model two-strategy evolutionary game dynamics (e.g., hawk vs. dove) from an initial strategy frequency and a specified pay-off matrix, tracking frequency trajectories to a frequency-dependent equilibrium in R. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/game-theoretic-payoff-matrix-analysis))
- **game-theoretic-socio-technical-control**: Game-theoretic frameworks for modeling, learning, and control in socio-technical systems. Activation: game-theoretic control, socio-technical systems, multi-agent resilience, incentive design, Stackelberg games, cooperative games, mechanism design ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/multi-agent-rl/game-theoretic-socio-technical-control) · ★4 · 4 copies)

- **game-theory**: Use when outcomes depend on other people's choices: why won't they cooperate, will people adopt it, who can veto, how will others respond, is that promise credible, or will this incentive backfire. - **game-theory**: Use when asked to analyze a decision whose outcome depends on what other rational parties will choose — identify the players, their options, payoffs, and information, then find dominant strategies, equilibria, and ways to change the game (commitments, repetition, side payments) — as distinct from [[expected-value-analysis]] (one decision-maker against uncertain nature), a [[decision-tree]] (no strategic opponent), and [[second-order-thinking]] (general downstream consequences without formal payoffs).
